景気の底堅さとインフレ圧力の根強さを確認。貿易
赤字はAI関連を含む設備投資需要などを背景に拡
大。非製造業PMIは拡大圏で推移する一方、仕入
価格指数の上昇は企業のコスト負担増を示唆

ユーロ圏総合PMIは節目の50超を維持。小売売上
高も小幅増加。ドイツの鉱工業生産は持ち直したが、
製造業受注は大型案件の反動で減少。景気は緩
やかな回復を維持するも、持続力には不透明感
欧
州
ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き
注目点② 日銀さくらレポートは企業活動の底堅さを再確認注目点①米欧で金利上昇。異なる背景と金融政策への含意

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
企業コメント概要
0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

げに伴う販売数量の減少も

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に
 今次局面では、現状、主要拠点への直接被害は確認されておらず、一部工場の生産停止にとどまっている模様
―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

（注） 旅行収支要因は、非居住者による国内消費（インバウンド消費）を控除し、居住者に
よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

### Page 1

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
中華民國一一五年九月七日

國際經濟金融週報

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國就業回穩支撐景氣韌性，惟通膨壓力與貿易逆差擴大增添政策不確定性 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國非農就業增加 16.2 萬人，優於前月及近一年平均，失業率維持
4.1%，就業市場呈現溫和增長，整體表現仍具韌性。新增就業以餐飲服
務、地方政府教育及製造業為主，惟資訊業仍減少人力。 
⚫ 7 月美國職位空缺數增至 727.1 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.3%升至 4.4%； 惟聘僱
及總離職人數分別減少 27.8 萬人及 26.5 萬人，其中主動離職人數減少
15.7 萬人，裁員與解僱人數減少11.9 萬人。整體來看，企業用人需求尚
屬穩健，但招募及勞工流動動能略有降溫，勞動市場供需仍維持平衡。 
⚫ 8 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.6，雖較上月下降 1.0 點，但
已連續八個月維持擴張。新訂單、就業、未完成訂單及進口指數回落，
顯示需求動能有所放緩 ； 惟生產與出口持續擴張 ， 供應商交貨時間延長 ，
原物料價格仍處高檔，整體製造業活動仍維持擴張態勢。 
⚫ 8 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數由 54.1 升至 55.4，連續第 26 個月
維持擴張。商業活動與新訂單指數分別升至 61.7 及 60.9，反映服務業
需求仍具支撐；惟雇傭指數連續兩個月低於 50，企業聘僱態度仍較審
慎。此外，價格指數升至 72.6，創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨與成本
壓力依然偏高。 
⚫ 7 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 886 億美元，較上月增加 24.4%，
主因出口減少 2.1%、進口增加 2.8%。出口下滑主要受原油及非貨幣黃
金等工業原料出口減少影響；進口則受電腦、半導體等資本財需求增加
帶動。累計前 7 月貿易逆差較去年同期縮減 29.6%，顯示美國對外貿易
失衡情況明顯改善。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 美國於 8 月 30 日以伊朗革命衛隊疑似準備在荷姆茲海峽部署水雷、威
脅商業航運安全為由，對伊朗拉拉克島軍事設施發動精準打擊。伊朗隨
後向約旦境內美軍基地發射飛彈及無人機報復，區域緊張情勢明顯升
溫。由於荷姆茲海峽為全球重要原油運輸樞紐，市場對能源供應與航運
安全的疑慮加深，帶動國際油價及避險需求走高，並增添全球通膨及金
融市場不確定性。

一、 美國—就業回穩支撐景氣韌性，惟通膨壓力與貿易逆差擴大增添政策不確
定性 
2026 年 8 月美國就業市場呈現回穩跡象，非農就業人數較上月增加 16.2 萬
人，不僅高於上月修正後的 2.1 萬人，也明顯優於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 3.1
萬人的水準；失業率則維持於 4.1%，失業人數約 703.1 萬人，顯示勞動市場整體
尚未明顯轉弱。從產業表現來看，新增就業主要集中於餐飲服務業增加 5.9 萬人
及地方政府教育部門增加 4.2 萬人 ， 製造業亦增加1.6 萬人 ， 持續支撐就業成長 ；
醫療保健業增加 1.3 萬人，雖維持擴張，但增幅低於過去一年平均水準。另一方
面 ， 資訊業減少2.3 萬人 ， 為主要拖累來源 ， 反映部分產業仍處於人力調整階段 。
勞動市場結構方面，勞動參與率回升至 61.6%，較前月改善，但仍較今年 1 月低
0.5 個百分點；就業人口比率則上升至 59.1%。薪資與工時方面，民營部門平均每
週工時升至 34.4 小時，平均時薪增至 37.75 美元，月增 0.3%、年增 3.1%，薪資
成長保持溫和。整體而言，8 月就業數據顯示美國勞動市場在前月疲弱後回穩，
且前兩個月數據上修，反映就業市場仍具韌性。強勁就業報告公布後，美債殖利
率走升，市場對聯準會 9 月維持偏緊政策的預期升溫，升息可能性亦有所提高。 
2026 年 7 月美國職位空缺數為 727.1 萬個，較上月增加 8.9 萬個，職缺率由
4.3%升至 4.4%；聘僱人數為 505.4 萬人，較上月減少 27.8 萬人，聘僱率由 3.4%
降至 3.2%；總離職人數為 507.2 萬人，較上月減少 26.5 萬人，離職率亦由 3.4%
降至 3.2%，顯示勞動市場流動性有所降溫。就離職結構觀察，主動離職人數為
305.6 萬人，較上月減少 15.7 萬人；裁員與解僱人數為 166.6 萬人，較上月減少
11.9 萬人 。 產業別方面 ， 職位空缺增加主要集中於耐久財製造業(增加 7.6 萬個)；
聘僱人數則以專業及商業服務業減少 18.8 萬人最為明顯 ； 主動離職人數以住宿及
餐飲服務業減少 8.6 萬人最多。整體而言，7 月美國職缺小幅增加，顯示企業用
人需求仍具支撐，惟聘僱及離職規模同步縮減，反映勞動市場流動性趨緩；加以
主動離職率及裁員率雙雙下降，顯示勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整均趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 8 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)為 54.6，較

處於擴張區間，但需求動能較前月放緩；生產指數由58.5 微降至 58.3，連續第十
個月擴張，產出活動維持穩健；就業指數由52.8 降至 51.2，仍處擴張區間，反映
企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由58.9 升至 59.3，供應商交貨時間持續

月美國製造業活動雖仍維持擴張，但擴張力道較前月略有減弱，新訂單、就業、
未完成訂單及進口等指標同步回落，反映需求成長動能趨緩；另一方面，供應商
交貨時間持續延長、原物料價格維持高檔，加以中東地緣政治衝突、關稅政策及
供應鏈不確定性仍存，可能持續對製造業活動形成壓力。 
2026 年 8 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 升至 55.4，較上
月 54.1 上升 1.3 個百分點，連續第26 個月維持在 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近12
個月平均值 53.7，顯示美國服務業景氣持續穩健擴張。商業活動指數由59.1 升至
61.7，創 2022 年 11 月以來最高水準；新訂單指數由 57.2 升至 60.9，連續 15 個
月維持擴張，創 2023 年 2 月以來新高，顯示需求動能進一步增強。勞動市場方
面，雇傭指數由 47.4 微升至 47.8，但已連續兩個月處於收縮區間，反映企業聘僱
態度仍偏審慎。價格指數由 70.3 升至 72.6，創 2022 年 8 月以來最高水準，顯示
通膨與成本壓力依舊偏高。供應鏈方面，供應商交期指數由52.8 降至 51.3，雖連
續 21 個月高於 50，但已連續第四個月改善；庫存指數則由 51.4 升至 56.7，顯示
企業備貨意願增強。此外，新出口訂單指數及進口指數均升至 56.3，反映外部需
求與採購活動同步回溫。整體而言，8 月美國服務業景氣擴張力道進一步增強，
商業活動與新訂單表現亮眼，惟就業指數仍低於榮枯線，加上企業持續關注關稅
政策及中東衝突對供應鏈與成本的影響，後續仍須觀察通膨與勞動市場發展。 
2026 年 7 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 886 億美元 ， 較上月增加174 億
美元，增幅 24.4%。當月出口 3,107 億美元，較上月減少 66 億美元(-2.1%)；進口
3,993 億美元，較上月增加 108 億美元(+2.8%)。其中，商品貿易逆差擴大至1,196
億美元，較上月增加 17.3%；服務貿易順差則增至 310 億美元，增幅 0.7%。出口

下滑主要受工業用品及原材料出口減少 87 億美元影響，其中原油及非貨幣黃金

進口則增加 1.9%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡情況較去年同期明顯改善。 
8 月 30 日美軍以伊朗革命衛隊(IRGC)疑似準備於荷姆茲海峽部署水雷 、 威脅
商業航運安全為由，對伊朗拉拉克島(Larak Island) 火箭發射設施發動精準打擊，
為美方逾一個月來首度對伊朗本土目標採取軍事行動。伊朗隨後以飛彈及無人機
攻擊約旦與阿聯酋境內美軍相關設施作為回應，顯示衝突已由雙邊軍事對抗逐步
向區域層面擴散。至 9 月 5 日美國中央司令部(CENTCOM)表示，因伊朗革命衛
隊向兩艘美國海軍艦艇發射彈道飛彈，美軍已對 3 艘伊朗油輪發動攻擊，其中一
艘位於伊朗重要石油出口樞紐哈爾克島(Kharg Island)外海。美方稱未造成人員傷
亡，惟伊朗半官方媒體指出，哈爾克島錨地附近油輪遭 4 枚美軍飛彈擊中，船員
已展開撤離。由於哈爾克島為伊朗最重要的原油出口據點，戰前約九成原油出口
均經由該地，加上荷姆茲海峽為全球關鍵能源運輸通道，本次衝突已明顯升高市
場對油輪航行安全、原油供應穩定性及運輸成本上揚的憂慮，帶動布蘭特原油期
貨於 9 月 4 日收報每桶 96.28 美元，創 7 月 24 日以來新高。整體而言，美伊衝突
已由原先針對軍事設施的交鋒，進一步延伸至能源運輸與油輪目標，若後續報復
與反制行動持續升級，恐加劇中東原油供應中斷風險，推升全球能源價格與通膨
壓力，並提高市場對美國經濟前景及聯準會貨幣政策走向的不確定性。 
二、歐元區—通膨壓力回升，核心通膨走向值得關注 
 歐元區 7 月失業率持平 6.4%，失業人數持平上月水準。其中德國失業人數增
加 1.1 萬人，失業率持平為 4%；法國、義大利失業人數皆下降 1 萬人，失業率分
別持平為 8.3%、5.8%； 西班牙失業人數持平上月 ， 惟因部分勞動力退出勞動市場 ，
使失業率略降為 10.1%。整體而言，失業率維持在 6.3%-6.4%的低點區間波動，
且 8 月 PMI 的就業指數也有所增溫， 顯示勞動市場的韌性可望延續， 並支持消費
需求表現。 
 歐元區 8 月 HICP 通膨上升 0.4 個百分點至 3.3%，其中因燃料價格上揚，能
源通膨上升 4 個百分點至 14.3%，食品通膨則穩定於 1.2%，迄今能源及化肥價格

流等成本管道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
三、日本—製造業產出穩健成長，消費動能仍具韌性 
7 月零售月增 2.4%，部份反映 6 月受梅雨及豪雨天氣影響後的回升，顯示消
費動能仍具韌性。其中耐久財消費持續受到政策及汰舊換新需求支撐，家電銷售
在 6 月大幅下滑後明顯回升 ， 主要受空調等產品需求帶動 ； 汽車銷售額大致持平，
顯示政府自 4 月推動的稅務優惠措施效果尚未消退。另，非耐久財消費亦呈現全
面改善，服裝銷售受炎熱天氣帶動而大幅回升，一般商品銷售則反映國內消費及
觀光需求同步增強，食品銷售亦維持成長， 惟部分增幅可能並非實際需求增加，
若未來食品通膨進一步升溫，恐壓縮家庭實質購買力及消費信心，進而對後續非
耐久財支出及消費動能形成壓力。 
7 月工業生產月增 0.1%，主要係人工智慧相關需求帶動，科技與非科技產業
產出同步增加，其中半導體製造設備、電子零組件及化學工業表現尤為亮眼。出
貨月增 2.2%，高於生產增幅，而庫存僅增 0.5%、庫存率下降 1.7%，顯示產品去
化情況良好，整體供需維持穩健。展望未來，企業預估 8 月工業生產將大幅成長
6.4%，雖 9 月可能略為回落，但PMI 等前瞻指標仍顯示製造業活動持續擴張，預
計第三季製造業產出可望維持穩健成長。 
四、中國—8 月景氣略為回穩，但前景仍有較多不利因素 
8 月官方製造業 PMI 回升 0.6 點至 49.8，連續兩個月處於微幅衰退區間，新
訂單、出口訂單指數雖分別回升至 50.6、50.1，並帶動生產指數回到 50.4，不過，
庫存指數下降 、 就業指數加速衰退 、 企業展望減弱 ， 皆拖累PMI 指數水準 。 此外 ，
供應鏈交期指數上升至 50.1、投入物價指數回升至 56.6，反映交期拉長及成本加
速上漲，使業者營運承壓；產出物價指數回升 2.6 點至 50.4，售價調整速度依然

較為遲緩，不利於獲利表現。另外，RatingDog 的 8 月製造業 PMI 則回升 0.6 點
至 50.9，出口訂單指數為近 6 個月最高，表現較官方製造業 PMI 為佳，反映
RatingDog 調查對象多為出口導向型企業所致。 
官方非製造業 PMI 持平 49，其中建築業活動指數降 0.1 點至 46.9，為連續八
個月處於衰退區間；服務業商務活動指數持平 49.3，非製造業的從業人員指數為
45.4，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，亦較不利於內需表現。另方面，RatingDog 服務業
PMI 增加 1 點至 51.4，較 7 月的低點溫和反彈。相較於官方非製造業 PMI，
RatingDog 服務業因不包含大型不動產開發商而表現較佳，且內需動能有回升跡
象，不過，RatingDog 的產出價格指數跌破 50，為今年首見，反映下游競爭激烈
導致成本難以轉嫁，故雖通膨可望維持低檔，但企業獲利將持續承壓。 
展望未來，G20 峰會將中國龐大貿易順差視為加劇全球經濟失衡的重要因素
之一，國際社會要求中國擴大內需及調整經濟結構的壓力持續升高。此外，中國
仍面臨美國 301 關稅調查、歐盟擴大貿易保護措施等，出口環境仍具不確定性。
未來將關注 9 月 24 日川習會，預計雙方將就關稅措施、出口管制、AI 及先進科
技管制等議題進行協商，若會談結果正向，將有助緩解中美貿易摩擦，降低出口
面臨之政策風險。 
五、印度及東南亞—印度經濟保持強勁、馬來西亞央行利率不變 
印度: 2026 年第二季(2026/27 財年第一季)經濟成長率 （GDP） 年增7.8%，持平於
上季，仍為二十國集團（G20）成長最快國家，成長最快行業依序為:金融服務業
12.1%、製造業 9.2%、公用事業 8.9%、貿易/旅館/運輸/通訊業 8.5%；不過，農業
產出僅增長 3.6%，採礦及採石業則收縮 2.4%。儘管上半年經濟成長表現強勁，
製造業、消費和信貸均展現韌性；不過，油價上漲、印度盧比承壓以及季風降雨
不足等風險持續積累 ， 並透過貿易逆差 、 通膨和企業成本等管道向實體經濟傳導 ，
為經濟前景增添不確定性。 
東南亞:8 月 S&P Global 東協 （ASEAN） 製造業採購經理人指數(PMI)較上月小幅
回落 0.5 點至 52.3，仍保持穩健擴張態勢，其中，新訂單以接近歷史高點的速度
增加，生產活動雖較前月放緩，仍保持穩健；採購活動持續擴張，但聘僱與原物
料庫存小幅下滑，顯示廠商在人力與庫存建置仍偏謹慎，未完成訂單持平前月，
顯示產能壓力依然存在。價格方面，投入成本與產品售價均降至六個月低點，通

膨壓力進一步緩和 ， 供應商交貨時間延遲情況較前月減輕 ； 企業信心進一步增強 ，
未來一年產出預期指數達近四年高點。按個別國家來看，8 月除印尼(49.8)自擴張
轉為收縮外 ， 馬來西亞(50.2)、 泰國(53.8)較上月下滑 ， 新加坡(59.4)、 菲律賓(54.9)、
越南(53.3)均加速擴張。整體而言，在需求穩健、供應鏈改善及價格壓力減輕下，
東協國家製造業可望保持穩健擴張態勢，惟美伊衝突的不確定性仍存，持續推升
通膨預期並干擾供應鏈，同時牽動全球資金流動，仍須留意其對生產及企業獲利
的影響。 
馬來西亞 ：馬來西亞央行 9 月貨幣政策會議 ， 如預期維持基準利率 （OPR）於2.75%
不變。央行指出，儘管全球經濟情勢存在不確定性，得益於強勁的出口、穩健的
就業與內需 ，2026 年上半年經濟成長率(GDP)達 5.7%， 預計2026 年全年可達 5%，
而良好基本面將使 2027 年的經濟成長保持韌性。通膨方面，2026 年前七個月，
平均整體通膨率和核心通膨率分別為 1.8%和 2%，儘管受中東緊張局勢影響，全
球大宗商品價格上漲，但預計物價壓力仍將保持在可控範圍內。整體而言，受益
於全球經濟前景的改善、科技相關出口需求強勁及旅遊業活動增加，為經濟成長
帶來上行潛力，惟地緣政治緊張局勢持續、全球金融環境趨緊以及對金融市場估
值過高的擔憂，仍帶來下行風險。目前的貨幣政策立場與物價持續穩定和經濟可
持續成長的前景相符 ， 未來將密切關注國際局勢變化 、 成本壓力和國內需求狀況 ，
彈性調整政策。

数据点评  
生产与消费 价格修复分化 ，内需 回暖 尚有 “时间差 ” 
——2026 年 9 月通胀数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院  
[Table_Summary]  摘要：CPI方面，9 月 CPI 同比上涨 0.9%，涨幅较上月扩大0.1个百分点，连
续两个月回升，但低于市场预期。食品价格同比降幅扩大至1.7%，主要受鲜
菜、 鲜果价格下跌影响， 猪价同比跌幅收窄； 非食品价格同比涨幅扩大至1.5%，
主要受国际油价反弹及电子产品价格上涨带动，其中汽油价格同比上涨
16.6%，拉动整体 CPI同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点。环比看，CPI 上涨 0.2%，
涨幅较上月收窄 0.2 个百分点，核心 CPI 同比涨幅持平于 1.0%，服务价格环
比转跌，反映国内终端需求修复仍偏温和。展望10 月，国际油价高位运行及
AI 算力需求带动的消费电子涨价仍将提供支撑， 但蔬果价格同比降幅扩大、 猪
肉价格环比转跌，叠加服务价格季节性回落，CPI 整体或延续温和修复。PPI
方面，9 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.0%， 涨幅较上月扩大1.2个百分点， 高于市场预期，
主要受国际油价反弹及低基数效应推动。分行业看，煤炭开采和洗选业、 石油
和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格同比涨幅分别扩大至
35.3%、24.5%和 21.6%，化工、化纤等行业价格亦明显回升，反映能源价格
上涨正沿产业链向中游原材料环节传导；电气机械、 计算机通信及其他电子设
备制造业价格环比均上涨0.4%，AI 算力相关需求形成一定支撑。 环比看，PPI
上涨 1.2%，涨幅较上月扩大 0.8 个百分点，石油相关行业价格上涨是主要拉
动。展望后续，美伊围绕霍尔木兹海峡的紧张局势、国际油价高位运行、冬季
煤炭备货及低基数效应仍将支撑PPI， 但上下游价格传导不畅、 终端消费偏弱，
生活资料价格上涨空间仍然有限，PPI 修复或延续结构性分化。 
 输入性因素支撑 CPI 同比修复，核心 CPI 保持稳定。同比来看，9 月 CPI 同
比涨幅扩大 0.1个百分点至 0.9%，连续两个月回升，增速低于wind一致预期
（1.02%）。其中，食品价格同比下降1.7%，降幅扩大 0.3个百分点。非食品
价格同比涨幅扩大 0.3 个百分点至 1.5%，主要受能源及电子产品价格涨幅扩
大影响。9 月中东地缘冲突持续发酵， 能源供应风险上升， 国际油价二次反弹，
带动国内能源价格涨幅扩大，对整体CPI 形成支撑。环比来看，9 月 CPI环比
涨幅收窄 0.2个百分点至 0.2%， 其中食品价格环比上升 0.4%，持平上月表现，
非食品价格环比涨幅收窄 0.1 个百分点至 0.2%。9 月核心 CPI 同比涨幅持平
于 1%， 核心CPI 环比回落至 0%。核心 CPI 连续数月运行在 1.0%附近， 说明
剔除输入性扰动后，国内终端需求的修复力度仍然温和。展望 10 月，国际油
价高位运行及 AI算力需求带动的消费电子价格上涨仍将为CPI提供支撑，但
食品价格仍面临一定下行压力。 同时， 暑期结束后服务价格进入季节性回落阶
段，核心 CPI 修复力度预计仍偏温和。整体来看，输入性因素仍将对CPI 形
成支撑，但内需修复偏缓或限制涨幅，预计10 月 CPI 同比延续温和回升。 
 食品价格同比降幅扩大，蔬果价格下跌是主要拖累。同比来看，9 月食品价格
同比下降 1.7%，降幅扩大 0.3 个百分点，主要受鲜菜、鲜果价格下跌及猪肉
价格仍处低位影响。 食品细项中，鲜菜、鲜果价格同比分别下降8.1%、1.5%，
降幅均扩大，反映季节性供给增加对农产品价格形成压制。牛肉、 羊肉价格同
比分别上升 7.5%、6.4%，涨幅均扩大。猪价同比下降 10.0%，降幅收窄 1.8
个百分点，价格弱势有所缓解，但同比仍处于较大跌幅区间。环比来看，食品
价格环比上升 0.4%，持平上月表现。食品细项中，鲜菜价格环比涨幅收窄5
个百分点至 0.5%，鲜果价格环比由下降转为上升 0.6%。猪价环比涨幅收窄
0.1 个百分点至 1.2%，9 月猪肉均价同比下降 13.58%，降幅继续收窄。从最
新的高频数据走势看，截至 2026年 10 月 13 日，同比方面，10 月初 28 种重
点监测蔬菜价格同比下降 17.1%，7 种重点监测水果价格同比下降 6.1%，同

 能源和电子产品支撑非食品价格， 暑期结束拖累服务价格环比表现。同比来看，
9 月非食品价格同比涨幅扩大0.3个百分点至 1.5%，主要受国际油价回升带动
能源价格上涨影响。9 月汽油价格同比涨幅扩大 7.3 个百分点至 16.6%，仅此
一项即影响 CPI同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点， 带动整体能源价格涨幅从4.1%跃
升至 7.3%。扣除能源的工业消费品价格上涨1.6%，涨幅回落 0.2 个百分点。

其中黄金饰品价格上涨 22.5%，涨幅回落 11.1 个百分点；数据存储设备、平
板电脑、 计算机和移动电话机价格分别上涨46.7%、23.6%、21.8%和 14.9%，
涨幅均有扩大，反映电子产品价格仍有较强支撑。服务价格同比上升 0.8%，
涨幅持平上月。环比来看，9 月非食品价格环比涨幅收窄0.1个百分点至 0.2%。
工业消费品价格上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.1 个百分点，其中国内汽油价
格上涨 4.9%，涨幅比上月回落2.3 个百分点， 黄金饰品价格由上月上涨7.6%
转为下降 2.1%。服务 CPI 环比由上升转为下降 0.2%。服务中， 随着暑期结束，
飞机票、 宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格分别下降8.7%、7.0%和 6.0%； 受成本走
高影响，移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格分别上涨 3.7%、
2.0%、1.7%和 1.5%。展望 10 月，非食品价格预计延续分化走势，同比仍受
到能源价格回升和电子产品涨价支撑， 但环比上涨动能可能进一步减弱。 一方

面， 国际油价同比改善以及电子产品价格上涨仍将支撑非食品价格表现； 另一
方面， 暑期结束后服务价格进入季节性回落阶段， 叠加黄金等前期涨价品类涨
幅收窄，非食品价格环比或继续放缓。
 油价反弹带动 PPI超预期回升，资源品价格韧性较强。从同比看，受国际油价
涨幅回升及低基数效应影响，PPI同比涨幅扩大 1.2 个百分点至 5.0%，增速高
于 wind 一致预期 （4.3%）。生产资料同比涨幅扩大1.5 个百分点至 6.5%，生
活资料同比下降 0.1%，降幅收窄 0.4 个百分点。分行业看，煤炭开采和洗选
业同比涨幅扩大 8.7个百分点至 35.3%，主要受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、 主产
矿区供应阶段性偏紧、国际煤价上涨预期效应等因素影响。石油和天然气开采
业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格同比涨幅分别扩大14.0、10.5 个百分点
至 24.5%和 21.6%。化学纤维制造业、 化学原料及化学制品制造业同比涨幅分
别扩大至 12.3%和 12.1%。一方面， 国际原油价格上涨直接推升采掘和炼化环

节价格；另一方面，石油作为基础原材料，通过燃料、化工品等渠道向中下游
传导， 带动化学原料、 化纤等行业价格上涨。从环比看，PPI环比涨幅扩大 0.8
个百分点至 1.2%， 生产资料环比涨幅扩大 1 个百分点至 1.4%， 生活资料环比
涨幅收窄 0.1 个百分点至 0.1%。分行业看，煤炭开采和洗选业环比涨幅扩大
6.7 个百分点至 9.5%；石油相关行业价格上涨动能显著增强。 石油和天然气开
采业、 石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格环比涨幅分别扩大4.3、4.9个百分点至
10.4%和 8.4%。电气机械和器材制造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业
价格环比均上涨 0.4%，AI 与算电协同带来的结构性需求推动资本开支扩张。
国际层面，10 月 13 日布伦特原油现货价格为130.92美元/桶，10 月初油价同
比涨幅扩大 28.36 个百分点至 100.08%。10 月以来，美伊围绕霍尔木兹海峡
的紧张局势持续升温，伊朗加强对海上运输活动限制，美国持续强化对伊朗石
油贸易的封锁，区域供应风险上升。原油市场供给担忧升温，国际油价或维持

高位，并通过能源、化工等产业链向国内工业品价格传导。9 月，PPI 同比涨
幅（5.0%）与 CPI 同比涨幅（0.9%）之间的差距达到 4.1 个百分点，工厂端
的原材料和能源成本正在快速上涨，但尚未同步传导至终端消费市场，中下游
企业的利润空间正受到挤压。后续来看，国际油价高位震荡、冬季煤炭备货叠
加低基数构成后续 PPI 的核心推升力量。石油与煤炭开采价格同比或继续走
阔，并向化工、化纤等中下游传导；AI 算力相关电气设备、通信设备制造价
格或维持增长。 但上下游传导仍不畅， 终端消费偏弱或仍制约生活资料涨价空
间。
 风险提示：国内政策落地节奏不及预期，国际大宗商品价格波动超预期， 海外
地缘扰动风险。

分析师承诺  
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规 渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明  
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准， 新三板市场以三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。  
公司评级  
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上  
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -10%与 10%之间  
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -20%与-10%之间  
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 -20%以下  
行业评级  
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%以下

Chenbro (8210.TW): Chassis and Racks in expansion on AI infrastructure 
upcycle; Neutral
12 October 2026 | 2:24AM HKT
We are positive on Chenbro’s leading market position in AI server chassis, riding on AI 
capex across US and China cloud service providers (CSPs), and expanding to full rack 
business (e.g. noise-cancellation racks, liquid cooling CDU racks, etc.). Chenbro’s 
revenues in Sep increased by 1% MoM, and we expect the sequential increase to 
continue in Oct-Dec, bringing 4Q26E revenue growth to +58% YoY / +44% QoQ. 
Catalysts in 4Q26 include: (1) major ASIC AI servers customers’ new models ramp-up, 
(2) rack business expansion on noise-cancellation racks ramping up and expansion to 
liquid cooling CDU racks, (3) AI servers powered by NVIDIA (e.g. HGX, MGX) in 
expansion with new chipset platforms, and (4) ongoing capacity expansion to 
support the revenues growth. Maintain Neutral on fair valuation.

### Page 2

Earnings revision

We lower net income by 8%/3%/4% in 2026-28E mainly on lower revenues to reflect the 
less-than-expected shipment of AI servers chassis and the 3Q26 revenues miss, which 
were 22%/15% lower than our estimates / Bloomberg consensus. We lower revenues by 
7%/5%/6% in 2026-28E.

Compared to Bloomberg consensus, we are 1%/12%/27% ahead on 2026E/ 27E/28E 
net income, reflecting our positive view on Chenbro’s revenues expansion.

### Page 3

Valuation

We continue to use near-term P/E to derive our 12m TP . We derive our target P/E 
multiple from (1) peers’ avg. ratio of trading P/E to forward year fundamentals (NI YoY 
and OPM), which is at 0.3x, and (2) Chenbro’s forward year fundamentals: 2027-28E NI 
YoY now at 47% on avg. and OPM is at 26.8% on avg. Our new target P/E multiple is at 
21.2x (vs. 20.2x of our previous TP implied 2027E P/E). Our previous target P/E multiple 
to forward year fundamentals was also 0.3x. Our new target P/E multiple of 21.2x is 
between the company’s avg.+1stv. P/E of 17x and peak P/E of 26x, reflecting our 
positive view on its expansion toward AI servers. Accordingly, we raise our target price 
by 1.7% to NT$1,507 (21.2x 2027E P/E) from NT$1,482.

### Page 4

Price Target Risks and Methodology: Chenbro 
Valuation: We use near-term P/E to derive our 12-month target price for Chenbro, 
consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of NT$1,507 
on a target P/E multiple of 21.2x on forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E of 21.2x is 
derived from the peers’ avg. ratio of trading P/E to forward year fundamentals (NI YoY 
and OPM). We have a Neutral rating on Chenbro on fair valuation. Key upside / 
downside risks: faster / slower-than-expected AI infrastructure investments, faster / 
slower-than-expected rack business ramp-up, or healthier / fiercer-than-expected 
competition.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 8 Oct 2026 close.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales +1% MoM/
+15% YoY
In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$65,400mn (+1% MoM/+15% YoY).
• 3Q26 sales came in at NT$197,059mn (+8% QoQ/+31% YoY), largely in line
with  our estimate at NT$201,379mn (+10% QoQ/+34% YoY), but slightly
below the consensus at NT$208,250mn (+14% QoQ/+39% YoY).

Our view:
• We believe the modest shortfall in 3Q26 revenue  vs. the market expectation
of ~10% QoQ, is due to some component supply shortages and the
customer's pull-in schedule.
• Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26 revenue to grow by double-digit % QoQ on
alleviated component supply and solid demand.
• Regarding the USITC Investigation No. 337, we believe near-term impact
should be manageable, but we will continue to monitor the developments on
this matter (see our report here).

• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
6274 台燿 
(Taiwan Union Technology Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/08 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收符合預期， 毛利率受 1H26 漲價提升 ， 預期 3Q26 毛利
將順利反應成本上漲與優化之產品組合 ， 毛利率預估提升 3%+ 。  
 AI 伺服器帶動高階銅箔基板需求強勁，同時驅動高階網路交換
器升級動能，銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸
續採用 CCL M8、M9 規格。 
 2H26 低軌 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火箭搭載低軌衛星，所採用之銅
箔基板升級至 M8 等級；以及，1.6T 光模組需求量增帶動銅箔
基板需求量增。

一. 2Q26 營收與財報符合市場預期，3Q26 營收優於預期  
台燿 2Q26 營收為 143.01 億元，+42.2%QoQ、+110.93%YoY，毛利
率為 29.72% ，稅後淨利為 23.42 億元，單季稅後 EPS+7.82 元。2Q26
台燿受到 ASIC CCL 專案出貨帶動，產品組合優化，毛利率已相較
1Q26 季增 4.5% 。台燿已公告 3Q26 營收為 179.48 億元(優於市場預
期 3%)，+25.50%QoQ，毛利率可順利轉嫁且高階 CCL 出貨比重提
升，預估台燿 3Q26 毛利率為 33.21%，稅後淨利 36.72 億元，單季
稅後 EPS+12.60 元。

二. AI 伺服器帶動 CCL/PCB 產值提升，產能擴充 33% 
宏遠投顧觀察，AI 伺服器與先進封裝等需求日增，高階 CCL 市場
需求將在 2024 年~2026 年 CAGR 達 26% ， 而高階 CCL 供給端 CAGR
則是 7%，高階市場需求殷切。高階銅箔基板(包含 AI 伺服器和高
階網路交換器，自 2024 年始採用 M8 CCL 材料；2026 年 M8 材料
佔總體 CCL 市場 40%，M8 材料佔比顯著高於 2022 年、2023 年的
22%和 26%。台系銅箔基板供應鏈當中，M6~M8 等高階材料的供
應廠商為(2383)台光電為首、(6274)台燿次之。回顧 3 年來，台燿
產能擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開
出，台燿總產能為 260 萬張/月，+13%YoY。展望 2027 年，台燿泰
國廠、常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120
萬張/月，預估 2027 年底時，總產能達 380 萬張/月，+46%YoY；預
估 2028 年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平
均 3 年每年產能擴充約 33% 。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交

換器(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿
市佔率得以提升 。 2027 年隨著 NVIDIA Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+ 、
M9 材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，
同業台光電與台燿高階 CCL 新產能釋出 ， 產品組合持續優化 。 2027
年起 ， 除一般型伺服器 Intel Diamond Rapids 平台 、 AMD Zen6 Venice
平台採用 PCIe Gen6 與 Ultra Low Loss CCL(M7)，PCB 板層數增至
20~24L；2027 年，隨著 1.6T Switch 逐步導入 M8/M9 CCL(HLC 層
數 46~52L、玻纖 Low Dk2/Q 布、銅箔 HVLP4/5) ，預期 CCL 廠商產
品規格顯著升級。

三.  台燿高階網路 Switch 板材、低軌衛星板材、1.6T 光
模塊板材，27 年規格朝向高階型號發展 
展望 2H26，在高階 Switch 升級方面，800G Switch 採用 M8、M9 
CCL 規格，2H26 台燿將新增高階 Switch 客戶，隨著 AI 伺服器、
高階網路交換器產業發展朝向 800G、1.6T Switch 升級趨勢帶動之
下， 
銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸續採用 CCL M8、
M9 規格。此外，2H26 開始 800G 與 1.6T 光纖收發模組(採用 M8、
M9 CCL)開始放量，AI 資料中心帶動光纖收發模組需求，400G 以
上光纖收發模組出貨量 2026 年 1 億個、2027 年 1.45 億個、2028 年
達到 1.6~1.65 億個；在中系系統廠商積極拉貨之下，1.6T 光纖收
發模組 2026/2027/2028 年估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M ， 1.6T 光纖
收發模組，於 2 年內呈倍數增長。低軌大型 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火
箭搭載低軌衛星 ， 所採用之銅箔基板升級至 M8 等級 ， 材料損耗要
求降低，訊號傳出完整性標準提高，對於 CCL、PCB(HDI)、玻纖
與銅箔等規格預期同步升級，推升材料成本與 PCB 產值。

四.預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 38.49/66.22 元 
2026 年受惠 AI 伺服器及 ASIC 需求持續強勁的帶動下，台燿為網
通、高階網路交換器銅箔基板重要供貨廠商，將受惠 AI Server 對
於高階銅箔基板強勁的需求，因而，預估台燿 2026 年營收 618.78
億元，+108.01%YoY，毛利率 31.13%，稅後淨利 114.79 億元，稅後
EPS+38.49元 。 隨泰國廠產能釋出 ， 預估台燿 2027年營收增至 963.61
億元，+55.73%YoY，毛利率為 33.26%，稅後淨利 198.23 億元，稅
後 EPS+66.22 元。

四.投資建議維持買進，目標價 2,150 元 
宏遠投顧認為，台燿營運重點在於，營運動能於 2026 年起加速啟
動，一般型伺服器 CPU 廠商 Intel 、AMD 均有新品帶動新平台升級
與相關供應的零組件商機，以及攻入 AI 伺服器、AI 高階網路交換

一. 公司簡介 
台燿(6274)為銅箔基板廠商，主要產製雙面印刷電路板與多層印刷
電路板之銅箔基板 、 內層銅箔基板 、 黏合片 ， 產品應用別當中 ， ELL
與 SLL(M7、M8)佔比 39%、VLL 與 LL(M4、M6)佔 31%、HDI 與 N-
LL 佔 16%、其他品項佔 14%。

二.2026~2027 年營運概況及展望 
1.HDI 與 M8 以上升級仍為關注焦點，2026~2028 年產能建置積極 
過往一般型伺服器用板採用 HDI 設計，強調細線路設計，CPU 尺
寸變大 Pin Count 數量增加至 2,000 只~4,000 只 Pin Count，PCB 板
面積不變之下要走向細線路化(線寬/線距)朝向更細線路設計。製
程上 HDI 經過多次壓合後，PP 在熱壓多次後，每一層熱膨脹係數
不同，導致通孔 Crack 或翹曲，製作時容易產生爆板，因此對於
PCB 板廠製作良率會是一大技術難關，而金像電、健鼎等廠商於
高階 HDI 相對具備生產優勢與競爭力，相對應台光電、台燿等高
階 CCL 供貨與升級也成為製程一大瓶頸；回顧 3 年來，台燿產能
擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開出，
台 燿 總 產 能 為 260 萬 張 /月，+13%YoY。展 望 2027 年，台 燿 泰 國 廠、
常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120 萬張/
月 ， 預估 2027 年底時 ， 總產能達 380 萬張/月 ， +46%YoY ； 預估 2028
年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平均 3 年
每年產能擴充約 33%。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交換器
(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿市佔
率得以提升。2027 年隨著 Nvidia Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+、M9
材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，台
光電與台燿等高階 CCL 新產能釋出，產品組合持續優化。

針對 AI GPU 與 ASIC 發展進程，Google TPU 出貨量從 2026 年 400
萬顆上修至 2027 年(V8 增量)增至 800 萬顆，倍數增長。AWS ASIC 
預計 2027 年出貨量達 330~350 萬顆，增長+21%YoY 。NVIDIA 體系
GPU，Rubin 系列層數提升至 24~26L，材料端從 M8/M8+往更高規
格升級，更高層數與多層式架構，促使 PCB 板訊號走線、供電完
整性、高速訊號之阻抗匹配控制、訊號傳輸完整性，皆帶動 PCB

[Table_Title] 
经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速

[Table_ReportType] 
——9 月经济数据跟踪

[Table_ReportDate] 2026 年 10 月 19 日

[T able_Summary] ⚫ 事件：2026 年Q3,GDP 不变价同比4.5%，环比1.2%。工业增加值
同比由5.2%上行至5.5%，固投累计同比持平于-7.2%，其中制造
业、基建、房地产投资分别由-2.3%、-4%、-19.9%变动为-2.1%、
-4.2%、-20.6%，社零当月同比由0.4%上行至1.1%。 
⚫ 三季度GDP 略高于市场预期，全年经济目标有望达成。2026 年三
季度GDP 不变价同比增速为4.5%，高于市场预期的4.4%。第二、
三产业对GDP 同比拉动作用边际放缓，第一产业对GDP 同比拉动
作用加强。工业产能利用率环比上行，采矿业整体小幅回升，制
造业整体小幅回落，行业分化明显。 
⚫ 工业生产季节性回升，出口拉动作用走强，高技术产业生产高位
边际放缓。工业企业出口交货值大幅拉升，出口给部分行业生产
带来较强拉动。采矿业生产持续修复，石油开采业为主要拉动，
煤炭产量跌幅收窄。制造业生产整体小幅回落，上游制造业中，
非金属矿、黑色金属冶炼行业生产上行，极端天气影响消退，施
工旺季带来需求回补，政策性金融工具加速落地，带来行业边际
改善。化工在地缘政策不确定性影响下，企业补库需求提升，行
业生产上行，有色金属冶炼及压延工业、橡胶和塑料制品业在价
格走高等影响下，生产同比回落。中游制造业表现分化，整体仍
处高位，消费品制造业整体回落。下游制造业中，汽车、运输设
备、电子设备制造同比均回落，新质生产力生产边际放缓，新旧
动能分化收敛。 
⚫ 服务业生产指数回升，生产性服务表现仍强。“双节”给服务业
带来一定提振，服务业活动预期指数持平。商务活动指数上行，
信息传输、软件和信息技术服务业与租赁和商务服务业同比小幅
上行，租赁和运输业同比均有所修复。 
⚫ 投资止跌企稳，资金投放和落地稳步推进。随着稳增长政策逐步
落地，固定资产投资逐步止跌企稳。设备购置在政策推动下持续
上行，建安工程继续下跌，新开工偏弱，投资项目不足的影响仍
存。固投资金来源来看，今年以来债券累计同比维持高位，政府
专项债用于项目建设比重明显提升，预算内资金同比跌幅有所收
窄，项目资金逐步落地。 
⚫ 地产投资持续下滑，销售依然偏弱。地产新开工、竣工跌幅加
深，施工增速持平。地产销售“金九”行情表现偏弱，二手房销
售好于新房。一线城市和部分二线城市新房价格环比上行，三线
城市价格依然偏弱，二手房价格同比多数下跌，房价依然有待企
稳。9 月 29 日居民购房贷款贴息政策公布，有望给刚需带来一定
利好，政策效果仍有待观望。房企资金来源持续收紧，销售回款
和融资环境依然承压，房企拿地偏谨慎。四季度土地供应总量收
缩的特征预期持续，结构逐步改善。 
[Table_Authors] 分析师: 于天旭 
执业证书编号： S0270522110001

邮箱： yutx@wlzq.com.cn

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 2 页 共 13 页  
⚫ 基建投资持续放缓，制造业投资反弹上行，财政资金有望给四季
度增速带来支撑。三季度以来财政资金投放有所提速，但落地进
程依然偏慢，基建下行斜率明显放缓。随着8000 亿元政策性金融
工具、政府专项债和地方政府债务结存限额在四季度投放并落
地，“六张网”建设进程有望提速，四季度基建将发挥“压舱
石”的作用。制造业投资上行部分对冲了地产、基建下行的压
力，高技术产业增速边际放缓。大规模设备更新政策、出口强韧
性，以及稳增长政策逐步落地给制造业带来拉动。 
⚫ 消费增速修复， “双节”带来提振，结构表现分化。服务消费好
转，餐饮增速上行，线上消费小幅放缓。居民实际可支配收入边
际上行，人均消费支出持平，居民消费信心窄幅震荡，内需整体
依然偏弱。可选消费结构分化，汽车为主要拖累，地产下游消费
偏弱。金银珠宝类本月跌幅收窄最为显著，通讯器材类消费持续
走高，假期出行需求提振下，石油及制品类跌幅收窄。必选消费
整体表现稳健，粮油、食品类、日用品类上行幅度较大。 
⚫ 四季度政策将发挥托底作用，关注政策落地效果。9 月我国经济
外需拉动、生产强、内需弱的格局持续，但新旧经济分化程度边
际收敛。9 月稳增长政策逐步加码，政府债发行提速，在政策扶
持下，四季度经济有望缓步回升，但仍需关注财政资金向实物工
作量的转化效率，预期全年经济节奏呈现“U 型”走势。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，居民购房持续低迷，我国
政策出台和落地不及预期

事件 
2026年三季度，GDP不变价同比增速4.5%， 季调环比增长1.2%， 前值0.9%。9月，工业
增加值当月同比增速上行0.3个百分点至5.5%，环比上涨0.61%，前值为0.54%。前三
季度，固定资产投资累计同比增速-7.2%，8月为-7.2%，其中，房地产开发投资累计
同比增速回落0.7个百分点至-20.6%，基建投资累计同比增速回落 0.2个百分点至-
4.2%， 制造业投资增速累计同比上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%。 社会消费品零售总额同比
增速由上月的0.4%上行至1.1%，

1    三季度经济增速略高于市场预期 
三季度GDP增速略高于市场预期，外需强劲拉动生产为主要支撑，全年经济目标有望
达成。2026年三季度GDP不变价同比增速为4.5%，市场预期为4.4%，一、二季度同比
增速分别为5%、4.3%；三季度环比增速为1.2%， 高于前值0.9%， 前三季度累计同比为
4.6%。分产业看，第一、二、三产业累计同比增速分别由上半年的3.7%、3.9%、5.2%
变动为3.7%、3.8%、5.2%，仅第二产业同比增速小幅放缓。第二、三产业对GDP同比
拉动作用边际放缓，第一产业对GDP同比拉动作用加强。二季度以来，地缘政治扰动
给全球经济、 物价带来较大风险， 我国出口保持强劲，给工业生产带来拉动， 为经济
重要支撑。 原油等大宗商品涨价压力加大， 输入性通胀被动抬升，我国工业品价格上
涨，CPI总体表现温和。 
工业产能利用率环比上行，行业分化明显。工业产能利用率小幅上行至73.1%，二季
度为73%。分行业来看，采矿业整体小幅回升，但仍低于去年同期，其中煤炭开采和
洗选环比上行幅度较大， 石油和天然气开采业小幅回落。电力、 热力、 燃气及水生产
和供应业环比回升。制造业整体小幅回落， 上游制造业中， 非金属矿物制品、 化学原
料及化学制品业产能利用率环比修复， 有色、 黑色金属冶炼及压延产能利用率同环比
下滑。中游专用设备制造产能利用率环比上行，同比高于去年同期， 通用设备制造环
比放缓，但仍处于高位，电气机械和器材制造环比上行明显。下游制造业表现分化，
计算机、 通信和其他电子设备产能利用率由78.7%上行至80.2%， 领跑其他行业； 汽车
制造业同、环比下跌幅度较大，医药制造业也有所回落。

2    工业生产回升，出口拉动作用较强 
季末月生产季节性回升， 出口拉动作用走强， 采矿业持续修复， 高技术产业生产高位
边际放缓。9月， 工业增加值当月同比增速上行0.3个百分点至5.5%，与市场预期的5.5%
一致，前三季度累计同比增速为5.3%。9月环比上涨0.61%，前值为0.54%。发电量同
比由上月的-0.8%回落至-1.2%。工业企业出口交货值当月同比由上月的11.1%上行至
17%，规模以上工业企业产销率当月同比由-0.1%下行为-0.2%。分三大门类看，9月，
采矿业同比上行2.7个百分点至1.3%，石油和天然气开采业本月同比上行幅度相对最
大，原煤产量同比跌幅收窄，天然原油产量同比上行，天然气产量同比由正转负。制
造业同比由上月的6.1%回落至6%； 高技术产业同比由上月的16.7%回落至15.3%。 电力、
燃气及水的生产和供应业同比上行0.7个百分点至5.6%。

图表3：采矿业持续修复，高技术产业高位放缓（%） 图表4：工业企业出口交货值增速攀升（亿元，%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

化工、 黑色金属行业生产上行， 下游制造业生产整体放缓， 消费品制造业整体回落。
细分行业来看， 上游制造业中，化学原料及化学制品制造业增速持续上行， 中东地缘
政治给供应链和原料价格带来较大扰动， 不确定性下企业补库需求提升， 乙烯、 化纤
产量上行。 非金属矿物制品、黑色金属冶炼及压延工业同比修复上行， 钢材、 水泥产
量同比跌幅收窄，极端天气影响消退后， 施工旺季带来需求回补， 政策性金融工具加
速落地， 叠加原材料成本上涨带来行业边际改善。 有色金属冶炼及压延工业、橡胶和
塑料制品业同比回落， 价格高位给生产带来一定抑制。中游制造业表现分化， 整体仍
处高位， 其中，通用设备、电气机械及器材制造业同比回落，专用设备制造业同比上
行。下游制造业中，汽车制造业同比回落2个百分点至6.7%，下滑幅度较大，高基数
带来一定影响；铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业同比回落1.7个百分点
至11.7%，增速持续放缓。计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业同比回落，工业机器
人产量同比下滑幅度较大，集成电路产量同比下行，微型电子计算机产量跌幅收窄。
消费品制造业中，医药制造业同比回落0.3 个百分点至 4.9% ， 农副食品加工业、食品
制造业生产增速放缓，纺织业同比小幅上行。

服务业生产指数回升， “双节”带来一定提振，商务活动也有所修复，生产性服务表
现仍强，服务业活动预期指数持平。服务业生产指数9月当月同比为4.8%， 较前值4.1%
显著上行， 中秋节和十一长假给服务业带来一定提振， 前三季度累计同比为4.7%，与
8月累计同比增速持平。服务业活动预期指数持平于8月的55.5%，当前居民消费依然
受到信心因素制约。 商务活动指数当月同比由49.3%上行至50.2%，信息传输、 软件和
信息技术服务业与租赁和商务服务业同比小幅上行，租赁和运输业同比均有所修复，
生产性服务依然相对强势。

3    投资：地产、基建延续磨底，制造业增速边际修复 
投资增速止跌企稳，资金投放和落地稳步推进，设备购置在政策推动下持续上行， 建
安工程继续下跌。2026年前三季度， 固定资产投资累计同比增速-7.2%，8月为-7.2%，
高于市场预期的-7.5%。固投季调环比增速为0.07%， 前值为-0.55%。随着稳增长政策
逐步落地， 固定资产投资逐步止跌企稳。其中， 房地产开发投资累计同比增速继续回
落0.7个百分点至-20.6%；基建累计同比增速回落0.2个百分点至-4.2%，制造业累计
同比增速上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%，民间固定资产投资回落0.3个百分点至-10.4%。
设备工器具购置同比增速由9.3%上行至9.7%，设备更新政策效果持续显现；建筑安装
工程同比由-9.8%回落为-10%，新开工偏弱，投资项目不足的影响仍存。第二产业投
资增速上行至主要支撑， 第三产业投资增速小幅回落， 第一产业下滑幅度较大。固投
资金来源来看，今年以来债券累计同比维持高位， 政府专项债用于项目建设比重明显
提升，预算内资金同比跌幅有所收窄，项目资金逐步落地。

3.1  房地产投资持续下滑，销售依然偏弱 
房地产投资增速持续下探，新开工、竣工跌幅加深。2026年9月，房地产投资累计同
比增速继续回落0.7个百分点至-20.6%，具体来看，房屋新开工面积累计同比增速回
落0.1个百分点至-24.9%，房屋施工面积累计同比持平于上月的-12.8%，竣工增速由
-23.7%回落为-25.1%。 
地产销售“金九”行情表现偏弱，二手房销售好于新房。9月，商品房销售面积累计
同比由-12.1%继续下探至-13.2%， 商品房销售额由-13%下跌为-13.9%， 房地产销售 “量”
的跌幅相对 “价” 更大。从70城房价数据来看， 一线城市新房和二手房销售价格环比
上涨， 二手房价格同比多数下跌，仅上海同比上行。 部分二线城市新房销售价格环比
上行， 三线城市新房、 二手房价格同环比持续放缓， 房价依然有待企稳。二手房交易
网签面积累计同比由10.6%小幅上行至10.7%， 二手房销售好于新房的格局持续， 但二
手房交易景气难以传导至房企回款与投资。商品房待售面积累计同比由-1.1%下跌至
-1.5%， 其中住宅累计同比由-0.5% 下行至 - 0.7% ， 地产库存持续去化。 8 月地产政策陆
续出台， 北京、 上海等核心城市购房调节进一步放松。 地产直至竣工验收具备交付条
件后方可收取购房资金和按揭贷款，优化了居民的购房环境，但从9月数据来看提振
效果相对有限。9月29日居民购房贷款贴息政策公布， 政策在10月1日期实施， 有望给

房企资金来源持续收紧， 销售回款和融资环境依然承压。房地产开发资金来源增速由
-21%回落为-21.6%。其中，个人按揭贷款跌幅小幅收窄，二手房交易活跃带来支撑，
定金及预收款增速与上月持平， 地产 “金九” 行情偏弱， 地产销售回款受限。 国内贷
款跌幅小幅修复上行， 自筹资金增速进一步下滑，地产行业信贷投放保持审慎， 房企
资金来源持续承压。 
房企拿地同比持续回落， 去库背景下， 房企依然偏谨慎。9月，100大中城市成交土地
占地面积环比季节性上行， 但同比由-16.1%继续小幅下调至-16.8%，房企拿地依然集
中在一线城市与核心城市的优质地块。房地产仍处去库进程当中，预售资金制度改革
影响将逐步显现， 房企资金周转受到约束， 叠加销售端和资金来源的限制，四季度土
地供应总量收缩的特征预期持续，结构逐步改善。

3.2  基建增速持续放缓，财政资金落地有望给行业带来托举

继续回落0.2个百分点至-4.2%， 三季度以来财政资金投放有所提速， 基建下行斜率明
显放缓， 但落地进程依然偏慢。 随着8000亿元政策性金融工具、 政府专项债和地方政
府债务结存限额在四季度投放并落地， “六张网” 建设进程有望提速， 四季度基建将

3.3  制造业投资反弹上行，高技术产业增速放缓 
制造业投资反弹上行，高技术产业增速边际放缓，出口和政策为主要拉动。9月，制
造业累计同比增速上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%，部分对冲了地产、 基建投资下行的压力，
民间固定资产投资回落0.3个百分点至-10.4%，依然表现偏弱；高技术产业累计增速
由5.2%回落至4.9%，设备工器具购置同比增速由9.3%上行至9.7%。 大规模设备更新政
策、 出口强韧性， 以及稳增长政策逐步落地给制造业带来拉动，上游资源品、 关键材
料等自主补库需求提高，也给制造业带来支撑。四季度随着财政资金投放陆续落地，
给制造业的支撑效果有望逐步显现。

4    消费增速修复，结构表现分化 
消费增速修复上行， “双节”带来提振，服务消费好转，线上消费放缓，内需整体依
然偏弱。9月， 社会消费品零售总额同比增速由上月的0.4%上行至1.1%， 市场预期0.7%，
累计同比持平于上月的1.1%。其中， 除汽车以外的消费品零售增速由上月的2.5%上涨
为3.9%， 上行幅度大于整体； 餐饮同比由上月的1.1%上涨为2.1%； 限额以上企业零售
同比由上月的-3.7%上涨为-2.1%。实物商品网上零售累计同比由4.3%回落为3.8%，网
上商品和服务占社零比重高位小幅下滑。城镇消费同比由0.2%上行至0.8%， 农村消费
由1.6%回升至2.7%，两者差距再度走扩。 
居民实际可支配收入边际上行， 人均消费支出持平，居民消费信心依然偏弱。2026年
前三季度， 全国居民人均可支配收入名义累计同比增长5.3%， 上半年为5.2%；扣除价
格因素实际累计同比4.4%， 前值为4.2%； 居民人均消费支出累计同比持平于上半年的
3%。从数据更新至8月的消费者信心指数来看，居民消费信心和意愿均小幅上行，但
收入信心指数持续回落。居民存款缓步回落， 就业和收入压力下， 信心有待进一步修
复。 
图表18：居民存款高位缓步回落（%） 图表19：消费者信心指数上行，收入信心指数回落

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

可选消费结构分化， 汽车为主要拖累， 地产下游消费偏弱， 必选消费表现稳健。可选
消费中，金银珠宝类本月跌幅收窄最为显著，黄金价格持续回落，高基数效应走弱，
给增速带来一定拉动。地缘政治影响下， 油价持续走高，在中秋和十一出行需求提振
下， 石油及制品类跌幅收窄。 汽车消费跌幅相对最大， 消费补贴和税费优惠政策退坡
带来一定影响。 地产下游行业中， 家电增速小幅放缓， 仍保持正增， 家具、 建材类跌
幅明显收窄。消费补贴对耐用品消费的透支作用逐步显现， 居民消费信心偏弱， 汽车、
地产后周期产品消费持续承压。 通讯器材类在高基数下， 增速持续走高， 新品发布周
期，内存价格大涨拉升了手机售价。必选消费增速均修复上行，其中粮油、食品类、
日用品类上行幅度较大。

5    失业率季节性回落，结构压力仍存 
失业率季节性回落， 农民工就业压力加大。9月，城镇调查失业率由5.3%下行至5.2%，
31个大城市城镇失业率也由5.3%下行至5.2%，外来农业户籍人口失业率持平于8月的
5.0%，BCI企业招工前瞻指数边际回落。9月就业人员平均工作时间由48.2小时/周上
行至48.3小时/周。从年龄结构来看，更新至8月的失业率数据中，16-24岁的年轻劳
动力失业率持续走高，25-29岁也有所上行，大学生毕业季带来的影响持续。随着大
学生就业逐步被吸纳，9月毕业季带来的影响季节性减弱。

6    总结与展望 
四季度政策将发挥托底作用，关注政策落地效果 。9月我国经济外需拉动、生产强、
内需弱的格局持续，但新旧经济分化程度边际收敛。9月稳增长政策逐步加码，政府
债发行提速， 在政策扶持下， 四季度经济有望缓步回升， 但仍需关注财政资金向实物
工作量的转化效率，预期全年经济节奏呈现“U型”走势。

7    风险提示 
地缘政治风险发展超预期，居民购房持续低迷，我国政策出台和落地不及预期。

Document 191: 9月美国通胀数据点评：通胀再度升温，年内加息预期强化

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月美国通胀数据点评：通胀再度升温，年内加息预期强化.pdf
Report source: 爱建证券
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告
总量研究 / 宏观研究 2026 年 10 月 19 日
细分研究
通胀再度升温，年内加息预期强化
——9 月美国通胀数据点评
相关研究
-
投资要点：
 事件：10 月 14 日美国劳工部公布通胀数据显示，9 月美国 CPI 同比增速达 3.7%，环比增速
达 0.6%。
 核心观点：9 月美国通胀反弹，能源价格上涨是主要推动因素，核心通胀则延续温和修复走
势。（1）能源价格大幅上行推高整体 CPI，住房及部分服务价格仍对核心通胀形成支撑。（2）
通胀水平走高，抬升市场对美联储年内加息的预期；另一方面，近期就业市场边际走弱，限
制美联储进一步收紧货币政策的空间。市场预期美联储 10 月维持利率不变的概率较高，12
月加息 25BP 的概率较高。
 总体通胀：能源价格大幅上涨，推动 CPI 上行。
 9 月美国整体通胀明显回升，主要受能源价格上涨推动，食品价格相对平稳。（1）能源价格上
升是推动通胀上行的主要动力。9 月能源价格环比上涨 5.2%，其中汽油价格上涨 9.1%，对

整体 CPI 涨幅贡献超过一半。受能源价格影响，整体 CPI 同比增速由 3.4%升至 3.7%。结构
看，通胀回升更多体现为能源价格冲击，而非各类商品和服务价格的普遍上涨。（2）食品价
格整体平稳，对通胀的推动作用有限。食品类价格环比持平，其中家庭食品价格小幅下降，外
出就餐价格有所上涨。食品价格总体保持温和，尚未形成明显涨价压力。总体看，CPI 上行主
因在于能源价格上涨驱动，能源价格是否继续高位运行，将影响后续通胀整体走势。
 核心通胀：涨幅温和，服务价格仍具粘性。
 9 月美国核心通胀小幅上行，价格压力仍未消退。剔除食品和能源后的核心 CPI 环比上涨
0.3%，同比由 2.4%涨至 2.6%。结构看，不同领域价格表现分化。（1）住房价格延续温和上
涨。住房价格环比上涨 0.4%，较前月略上行。住房价格在核心 CPI 中占比较高，显示核心通
胀仍具备一定上行支撑。（2）部分商品和服务表现分化。新车、服装、交通服务等价格上行，
推动核心 CPI 维持正增长。二手车和卡车、医疗保健商品价格有所下降，显示核心通胀尚未

全面加速上行。整体而言，核心通胀仍处于温和上升区间，但住房和部分商品与服务价格粘
性，使通胀回落面临一定阻力。
 政策预期：通胀上行抬升加息概率，年内紧缩预期强化。
 通胀上行抬高市场对美联储加息的预期，就业市场走弱则限制加息力度。（1）通胀上升增强
年内加息必要性。整体 CPI 与核心 CPI 同比同步上行，通胀水平离美联储目标仍有差距。若
能源价格持续维持高位或上涨，并传导至核心商品和服务，美联储则继续维持偏紧的政策立
场，通过加息抑制通胀。（2）就业走弱限制加息力度。新增非农就业下行，失业率上行，劳动
市场走弱，降低美联储大幅加息的可能。通胀上行叠加就业走弱，市场预计美联储在 10 月议
息会议上仍保持利率水平不变，12 月大概率加息 25BP，年内加息概率强化。
 总结而言，9 月美国通胀上行主因在于能源价格上涨推动，核心通胀则延续温和走势。能源
价格大幅上行抬升整体通胀压力，住房及部分商品和服务价格对核心通胀形成支撑，通胀回
落仍面临阻力。通胀上行，美联储年内加息预期强化；同时，就业走弱制衡美联储大幅紧缩。

 风险提示：美国就业数据超预期，美国通胀水平超预期，地缘冲突风险。
证券分析师
陈国文

投资建议的评级标准
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为股票评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内的相对市场表现，也即：
以报告发布日后的 6 个月内的公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅作为基准。其中：A 股市场：沪深 300
指数（000300.SH）；新三板市场：三板成指（899001.CSI）（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（899002.CSI）（针对做市转让标的）；
北交所市场：北证 50 指数（899050. BJ）；香港市场：恒生指数（HIS.HI）；美国市场：标普 500 指数（SPX.GI）或纳斯达克指数（IXIC.GI）。
股票评级
买入 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅大于 15%
增持 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 5%～15%之间
持有 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-5%～5%之间

卖出 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅小于-5%
行业评级
强于大市 相对表现优于同期相关证券市场代表性指数
中性 相对表现与同期相关证券市场代表性指数持平
弱于大市 相对表现弱于同期相关证券市场代表性指数

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Document 253: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-20)

Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-20

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

2 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

6 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Acter, an established 
multinational cleanroom engineering, procurement, construction and 
management (EPCM) turnkey service provider, with a Buy (1) rating. Its 
core business includes cleanroom electromechanical engineering, 
water/gas/chemical delivery systems, and other cleanroom services. We 
believe now is a good time to revisit the stock as it underperformed the 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly on weaker-than-expected 2Q26 
results and soft sector sentiment. Given its stronger revenue momentum 
over recent months and upcoming TSMC results on 15 October, which may 
drive sentiment, we suggest investors to buy the stock. We forecast a 
24.6% revenue CAGR and 36.9% net profit CAGR over 2026-28E.

Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, 
and rapid cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order 
backlog. Its backlog reached TWD66bn in July 2026 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 
and TWD30bn in 2022 (15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the 
global cleanroom technology market’s forecast CAGR over 2023-2032E, 
according to Precedence Research.

Strong competitive edge supports order share gains across multiple 
segments. Acter’s competitive edge lies in 1) intelligent management 
enabled by digital twins and AI, using 3D building information modelling 
(BIM) and AI-driven digital twin management; 2) intellectualisation of green 
engineering, which comprises green design, green procurement, and green 
techniques; 3) experience across multiple industries (ie, data centres and 
electromechanical engineering for the consumer industry) which supports 
better integration; and 4) a complete network, enabling on-the-spot support. 
Acter believes it has the highest market share among the operators of three 
major US data centre players due largely to its capabilities in liquid cooling 
and its expertise in electromechanical and cleanroom engineering.

Catalysts: Near-term: project wins from key clients, supporting stronger 
monthly revenue momentum; mid- to long-term: an improving operating-
margin trend and a resilient order backlog.

Valuation: We are the first non-domestic broker to cover Acter. We initiate 
coverage with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target PER of 20x 
(equivalent to a 0.54x PEG over 2026-28E). We believe our valuation is 
fair, given Acter’s strong earnings visibility, which we believe warrants a 
rerating. Our TP is supported by a DCF valuation of TWD1,454.

Risks: 1) weaker-than-expected expansion in the semiconductor industry; 
and 2) project delays or cancellations.

7 October 2026

Acter

Initiation: acting on the demand for cleanrooms

➢ Key beneficiary of the cleanroom shortage in the high-tech industry 
➢ Fast-growing order backlog likely to be realised over 2026-28E 
➢ Initiating with a Buy (1) rating and a 12-month TP of TWD1,415

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

Acter: net profit and net-profit growth    
We forecast Acter to record a 36.9% net profit CAGR over 
2026-28E, on the back of a 24.6% revenue CAGR over the 
same period, driven by 1) larger project scale, as strong 
demand for cleanroom EPCM services from the high-tech 
sector is likely to support continued growth in Acter’s order 
backlog and 2) improving gross margins and operating 
efficiency. We expect operating margin to expand by 2.0pp 
in 2027E and 1.3pp in 2028E, driven by favourable gross 
margins on new projects and operating leverage.

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Valuation   Acter: 1-year-forward PER bands    
Our 12-month TP of TWD1,415 is based on a target PER 
of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Our 
target multiple of 20x is higher than the stock’s past-3-year 
1-year-forward PER (12.2x) and past-5-year 1-year-
forward PER (9.6x) and implies a PEG of 0.54x on our 
36.9% net profit CAGR forecast over 2026-28E. We 
believe the stock deserves a rerating in the next 12 
months, largely supported by resilient cleanroom demand 
globally.

Our TP is also supported by an alternative DCF-derived 
valuation of TWD1,454/share.

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

Earnings revisions

Acter: Bloomberg consensus EPS forecast revisions   
Bloomberg consensus EPS estimates for 2026E and 
2027E have risen 24.8% and 24.3% YTD, respectively. 
This is likely due to upward revisions to order backlog and 
earnings forecasts following better-than-expected industry 
outlook. We are the first foreign broker to cover Acter.

Source: Bloomberg

Company profile 
Established in 1979, Acter Group (Acter) is a leading engineering services provider specialising in 
cleanroom, electromechanical, and high-tech facility integration solutions for semiconductor, 
optoelectronics, biotech, green energy, and advanced manufacturing clients across Taiwan, China and 
the rest of the world. As of 2025, its revenue comprised cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas 
and chemical delivery systems (20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and 
high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

### Page 5

5

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acting on the demand for cleanrooms 
Why are cleanrooms a bottleneck in the high-tech 
industry? 
In recent years, cleanrooms have become a critical bottleneck for high-tech industries, 
especially semiconductor manufacturing, OSAT, AI hardware, and PCB, mainly because of 
its indispensable nature, severe supply-demand imbalance, labour shortages, and high 
technical and regulatory barriers.

Cleanrooms are an indispensable core infrastructure as they determine product 
yield and quality stability. Advanced manufacturing nodes in semiconductors, 
optoelectronics, and biopharmaceuticals require highly particle-free, constant-temperature, 
and constant-humidity environments to ensure reliable production. As process 
technologies continue to advance, such as 2nm node (or even newer nodes) and 
advanced packaging, existing cleanrooms must be rebuilt, and the complexity of building 
new ones thus increases. This keeps overall demand for cleanrooms at high levels.

A severe global supply-demand imbalance with demand for cleanroom construction 
outpacing supply. The surge in AI servers, high-performance computing (HPC), and high-
bandwidth memory (HBM) has triggered a global expansion for front-end wafer fabs, 
packaging and testing facilities, and PCB manufacturing. Order visibility remains extremely 
strong across the industry. According to Markets and Markets, the global cleanroom market 
size is set to expand at a CAGR of 6.6% over 2025-31E. At the same time, the global 
semiconductor cleanroom market size is set to grow at a CAGR of 8.0% over 2025-30E. 
For instance, Acter’s order backlog exceeded TWD66bn at the end of July 2026, up from 
TWD46bn at the end of 2025, showing the urgency of demand for cleanroom construction.

Global: cleanroom market size and growth      Global: semiconductor cleanroom market size and growth

Source: Markets and Markets  Source: Markets and Markets

Human resources are an additional limitation to capacity growth. Cleanroom 
engineering is highly technical and labour-intensive, relying on specialised on-site 
installation and supervision that cannot be replaced by robots. At the same time, the 
industry faces severe labour shortages and an aging workforce, and younger generations 
are reluctant to join field operations. Cross-border staff deployment is also less cost-
efficient than local hiring.

Cleanrooms involve complex system integration, creating high barriers to entry. A 
cleanroom construction includes 4 sequential phases, where each phase usually requires 
totally different areas of expertise. Therefore, the competitive landscape in each area 
differs. The process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the physical 
plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for essential utility 
generation, where Acter is one of the leading global experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
network and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. As

### Page 6

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
global supply chains restructure under N+1 (domestic market + 1) strategies, companies 
building overseas facilities in places like the US face strict and complicated licensing 
processes, particularly on fire safety and hazardous chemicals. Without experience dealing 
with such engineering and safety issues, construction timelines can be severely delayed.

As a result, even with advanced equipment and chip designs, a high-tech company cannot 
begin production without adequate cleanroom space, skilled engineers, and system-
integration capabilities. Therefore, cleanroom engineering contractors hold significant 
bargaining power and capacity allocation leverage in today's tech supply chain.

IC foundry 
In the foundry sector, where the cleanroom supply-demand shortage is the tightest, project 
activity remains centred on advanced process capacity expansion in Taiwan and Arizona. 
Simultaneously, domestic advanced node development remains intense, with multi-phase 
fab builds advancing across Hsinchu, Taichung, and Kaohsiung. Local cleanroom and MEP 
integration demand is further boosted by advanced packaging facilities in Taiwan (such as 
Chiayi and Tainan) and upcoming projects in the US are set to contribute revenue for the 
cleanroom providers in the following years.

Memory 
The memory segment is exhibiting robust momentum as well, mainly catalysed by HBM 
capacity additions. Projects such as the plant in Japan, transitioned to contribution for the 
cleanroom EPCM providers starting from 2026. In Taiwan, turnkey engineering contracts in 
Miaoli are also actively underway. This trend is reinforced by stronger-than-expected 
expansion activity from memory producers in China.

OSAT,  PCB and ODM/OEM 
For OSAT companies, demand is also surging due to the spill-over effect of advanced 
packaging protocols and N+1 supply chain restructuring. The OSAT names are expanding 
their footprint both in Southern Taiwan and in overseas locations including in the US and 
Southeast Asia. Major engineering contracts signed between OSAT clients and Acter show 
stable long-term workload for strong cleanroom demand over our forecast horizon. 
Similarly, overseas expansion for PCB manufacturing is peaking in Southeast Asia, 
especially in Thailand. We expect Chinese PCB players to also increase their capacity, just 
like memory companies in the past year. Except for front-end and back-end chip 
manufacturing, ODM/OEM and data centre infrastructure is also a key growth engine for 
the EPCM industry. Major Taiwanese ODMs are building production facilities in Texas and 
Mexico to meet local manufacturing needs. Cleanroom contractors, such as Acter, 
providing MEP and system integration in Texas are directly benefiting from these projects. 
The shift toward liquid cooling systems has also increased engineering complexity, 
favouring few cleanroom companies with the expertise to deliver complex integration for 
CSPs in both North America and Southeast Asia.

Acter: an integrated cleanroom solutions provider  
Acter Group (Acter), an integrated cleanroom solutions provider, maintains its competitive 
positioning within the high-tech cleanroom and MEP engineering sector, driven by its group 
synergy and global footprint. Acter operates as a comprehensive vendor through a group 
business model. The parent company, Acter, supervises overall cleanroom and MEP 
services, while subsidiary Nova Technology (Novatech, 6613 TT, not rated) mainly provides 
chemical primary system and hook-up, and Novatech’s subsidiary Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated) provides gas primary system, hook-up and equipment. In China, Acter operates 
through Acter Technology Integration Group (603163 CH, not rated) and Suzhou Winmax 
(not listed) for Chinese EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management) 
and equipment business. Except for C&S (civil and structural) works, where the margin is 
low (7-8%) and the contract duration is long, Acter’s services span nearly the entire 
cleanroom construction process. Its one-stop solutions model supports customer retention 
Acter provides

solutions via various 
subsidiaries

and improves bid-win rates, particularly for overseas expansion, where customers have 
few reliable providers to choose from.

Acter: organisational chart

Source: Company, Daiwa

To avoid the risk of over-concentration, Acter diversifies its client portfolio across 
semiconductor foundries, memory, PCB, OSAT, ODM hardware assembly, CSP data 
centres and the consumer industry. By sector, semiconductor projects represent the largest 
share of Acter’s portfolio, accounting for 66% of total revenue in 1H26, vs. 69% in 2025. 
Foundry manufacturing is the largest source of demand for cleanrooms in Taiwan, 
especially as advanced-node capacity expands at sites in Hsinchu, Taichung and 
Kaohsiung. In the memory segment, the company executes turnkey engineering builds to 
expand its capacity aggressively, creating cleanroom opportunities across Taiwan, Japan 
and Singapore. This multi-sector coverage decreases the risk of cyclical downturns in any 
individual segment, in our view.

By establishing 33 operating entities across 11 international regions, the company 
effectively captures capex migration driven by global N+1 supply chain diversification. This 
allows Acter to support Taiwanese and international clients as they expand manufacturing 
capacity from Taiwan into Southeast Asia, Japan and the US. Due to the complexity of 
advanced-node IC manufacturing, packaging and testing, it is especially important for 
these Taiwanese semiconductor companies to work with familiar cleanroom builders when 
expanding in the US. This is one of the reasons why Taiwanese cleanroom companies are 
receiving overseas orders and starting to set up global bases, in our view. Despite higher 
management, material, and manpower costs in the US, most cleanroom companies are 
benefitting from the trend by improving margins, given the higher complexity and risks to 
operate overseas. Across overseas hubs in Texas and Phoenix (expected to begin from 
2027), Acter prioritises local hiring and partners with regional civil contractors to shorten 
the learning curve and to avoid the high overhead. To further address the potential labour 
shortages in the future, the company has established cross-border academic and 
vocational partnerships to bring Southeast Asian talent to Taiwan for technical training 
before deploying them back to their home markets, securing future human capacity to 
support global expansion projects.
